|:---|:---|
| **Northwest and West Germanic**<br>[no change]<br><br>**Early Anglo-Frisian**<br>[no change] | **Old English**<br>OE I Umlaut: *fȳri<br>OE High Vowel Apocope: *fȳr |



### Orthography & surface

Outcome: fȳr



### Lexeme report

#### Reconstruction and comparative evidence

Kroonen places the lexeme in a heteroclitic family [fōr]{.recon} 'fire' ~ _\*fun-_ and explains
the front-mutated West Germanic forms from an oblique form of the
_\*fu(w)eri_ type [@Kroonen2013, 151]. The derivational input [fūri]{.iv .recon lang=pgmc sort=furi role=selected_input source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:23" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:23:1"} 'fire' is CAPR's oblique-input
proxy supplying the _i_ needed for i-umlaut. The account does not
independently certify that exact complete word, its long *ū*, or its cell.

The Old English target combines a regular inherited form
[_fȳr_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyr role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:28" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:28:1"} 'fire' with an attested oblique surface form [_fȳre_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyre role=target_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:28" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:28:2"} 'fire', interpreted by CAPR as analogically restored.

#### Competing ancestry and the legitimacy of the input

Kroonen distinguishes the dative \emph{*fu(w)eni}, its *r*-remodelled
\emph{*fu(w)eri}, and metathesized \emph{*feuri}. His preferred
pre-Germanic nominative is \emph{*péh₂-ur}; the \emph{*ph₂-uōr}
proposal reported from Simms is rejected, not endorsed
[@Kroonen2013, p. 151]. Ringe instead supplies a reaccented
zero-grade collective, printed \emph{*ph2uōŕ}, and separately considers
post-Proto-Germanic oblique dissimilation and *n* replacement by *r*
[@Ringe2017, pp. 147, 309]. These are consequential formation
and accent alternatives, not interchangeable quoted dative inputs.

Ringe and Taylor reconstruct apparently disyllabic West Germanic
\emph{*fuir} with *r* levelled into the oblique stem, but doubt that
the endingless dative evidence establishes inherited \emph{*fuiri}.
A front-rounded intermediate in the history of *fȳr* is plausible;
the intervening stages remain uncertain
[@RingeTaylor2014, pp. 119, 225]. Neither passage endorses CAPR's
exact long-*ū* proxy. Equal citation and input fields select the
Proto-Germanic stage by project convention, not by a new scholarly
quotation. Quantity, cell and historical input legitimacy require
separate scientific assessment; no input migration is made here.

#### Old English evidence

Toller's Supplement records [_fyr_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyr role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:55" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:55:1"} 'fire' as a noun in its _fyr. Add_ entry and separately preserves oblique
[_fyre_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyre role=target_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:56" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:56:1"} 'fire', including _in fyre_ [@Toller1921, 276]. These attestations establish the forms, not the date or mechanism of restoration. The first is the
regular output of the phonological development from the selected
input; the second is interpreted by CAPR as restoration of final _-e_
within the paradigm, rather than demonstrated as such by the dictionary.

The entry therefore distinguishes a regular path from an adopted input
proxy, the comparative legitimacy of that proxy, and an attested surface
form whose additional inflection CAPR treats as morphological remodeling.

#### Development to Old English

From [fūri]{.iv .recon lang=pgmc sort=furi role=selected_input source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:67" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:67:1"} 'fire', i-umlaut changes _ū_ to _ȳ_ [@HoggPhonology1992, §3.3.3.1]. Subsequent
loss of the final high vowel after a heavy syllable yields [_fȳr_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyr role=regular_output source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:68" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:68:1"} 'fire'
[@Campbell1959, §345]. The selected phonological path is complete at that point.

CAPR interprets the final _-e_ of [_fȳre_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyre role=target_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:71" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:71:1"} 'fire' as
analogical restoration in the Old English paradigm rather than retention of
the derivational input's ending. The form therefore remains a known but unmodelled
remodelling: the deterministic phonology is regular, but the attested surface
form includes later morphological rebuilding.

#### Paradigm comparison

The comparison below sets the relevant forms side by side. It distinguishes the lexeme-level etymological
background from the inherited cell that actually produces the front-mutated
form and from the later analogical surface result.

| PGmc cell / interpretation | Candidate input | OE output or comparison | OE comparison form | Result |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| lexeme-level heteroclitic headword | [fōr]{.recon} 'fire' ~ _\*fun-_ | comparative background only | fire family | explains the wider lexeme, but not the selected oblique input |
| selected oblique-input proxy | [_\*fūri_]{.iv lang=pgmc sort=furi role=selected_input source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86:1"} | regular output: [_fȳr_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyr role=regular_output source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86:2"} 'fire' | [_fȳr_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyr role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:86:3"} 'fire' | regular output; exact ancestral cell remains qualified |
| later analogical surface form | — | attested [_fȳre_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyre role=target_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:87" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:87:1"} 'fire' with restored _-e_ | [_fȳre_]{.iv lang=oe sort=fyre role=target_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:87" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2013-fire-fȳre.model.md:87:2"} 'fire' | genuine OE target, but not the direct phonological output |
# tap
PROTO: *táppô
EXPECTED: tæppa
OUTPUTS: tappa



### Proto-Germanic consonant inheritance

English Proto Input: *táppô

| Earlier Germanic developments | Old English developments |
|:---|:---|
| **Northwest and West Germanic**<br>[no change]<br><br>**Early Anglo-Frisian**<br>[no change] | **Old English**<br>EAF Brightening: *tæppô<br>OE A Restoration: *tappô<br>OE Unstressed Long Vowel Shortening: *tappa |



### Orthography & surface

Outcome: tappa



### Lexeme report

#### Reconstruction and early-stage alternatives

The aligned row keeps both `PROTO` and `PROTOFORM` as `*táppô`, because the project is not claiming that a different inherited protoform solves the Old English target. Instead, the note says the opposite: no paradigm cell of the inherited nominal paradigm yields lautgesetzlich `tæpp-`. This makes `tap` a good pilot for the distinction between a known historical explanation and a form the deterministic FST should not be forced to derive.

#### Chronological source dossier

- `Germanic/data/germanic-aligned-final.tsv`: the note classifies `tæppa` as an analogical n-stem levelling case and cites [@Orel2003], [@Kroonen2013], and [@Fulk2018].
- `Germanic/docs/DEV_NOTES.md` §17.10.16a-c argues that nominal cells keep back-vowel environments at the relevant stage, while the co-radical weak-verb pathway would produce `tepp-`, not `tæpp-`.
- The row is therefore one of the clearest examples of a historically intelligible but unmodellable analogical result in the present cascade.

#### Old English philology

The target `tæppa` is a genuine Old English noun form. The issue is not its attestation but its historical derivation: the project notes treat the `æ` as analogical and explicitly reject any claim that it is the direct phonological continuation of an inherited Proto-Germanic noun cell.

#### Project problem and solution

This is a `known_unmodelled` case. The FST's regular output from the nominative singular is `tappa`, and representative oblique cells remain in the same back-vocalic zone. Since no inherited cell gives `tæppa` by regular sound change, the row is correctly left as a documented exception rather than retargeted to a misleading pseudo-solution.

#### Paradigm probe

### Paradigm probe — tap / tæppa

- PROTO: *táppô
- PROTOFORM: *táppô
- DERIVATION_CLASS: known_unmodelled
- Morphology source: Hand-specified pilot comparison for n-stem singular cells drawn from DEV_NOTES and oe_known_problems.tsv.
- ProtoGate bypassed: no
- Generated cells: nom.sg., gen./dat./acc. stem
- Omitted cells: Plural cells omitted in v1; the ledger already states that no paradigm cell yields lautgesetzlich tæpp-.
- Winning form unique: no

| Cell | Candidate input | FST output | Match? | Comment |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| nom.sg. | *táppô | tappa | no | TSV input; ledger says this yields regular tappa. |
| gen./dat./acc. stem | *táppan | tappan | no | Representative oblique-stem comparison from DEV_NOTES. |

# buck
PROTO: *búkkaz
EXPECTED: bucc
OUTPUTS: bocc



### Proto-Germanic consonant inheritance

English Proto Input: *búkkaz

| Earlier Germanic developments | Old English developments |
|:---|:---|
| **Northwest and West Germanic**<br>PNWGmc U Lowering: *bókkaz<br>EAF Final Z Deletion: *bókka<br><br>**Early Anglo-Frisian**<br>[no change] | **Old English**<br>PWGmc Final Bare A Loss: *bókk |



### Orthography & surface

Outcome: bocc



### Lexeme report

#### Reconstruction and early-stage alternatives

For `buck`, the project does **not** distinguish `PROTO` from `PROTOFORM`: both remain `*búkkaz`. The row note says explicitly that no lautgesetzlich paradigm-cell rescue is available. Low-vowel cells trigger a-umlaut and high-vowel cells trigger i-umlaut, so the attested form cannot be recovered by simply choosing a different inherited cell.

#### Chronological source dossier

- `Germanic/data/germanic-aligned-final.tsv`: the row is marked as a documented exception and cites Campbell's treatment of `bucca` as an exception to a-umlaut.
- `Germanic/docs/DEV_NOTES.md` groups `*búkkaz`, `*wúlfaz`, and `*wúllō` together as the remaining labial-neighborhood `u`-retention exceptions.
- The row note also frames the problem against the regular a-umlaut environments described in [@Campbell1959] and [@Stiles2012].

#### Old English philology

The target `bucc` is treated as a genuine Old English form. The project is not questioning the lexeme identity or the attestation; it is questioning whether the deterministic inherited sound changes can produce it. The current answer is no.

#### Project problem and solution

This is an `unexplained_unmodelled` case. The FST gives the regular result `bocc`, which matches the comparative expectation better than the attested OE form does. Because no different inherited cell yields `bucc` without creating a different, equally wrong umlaut pattern, the report should describe the exception candidly and not pretend it has been solved.

# rust
PROTO: *rústaz
EXPECTED: rust
OUTPUTS: rost



### Proto-Germanic consonant inheritance

English Proto Input: *rústaz

| Earlier Germanic developments | Old English developments |
|:---|:---|
| **Northwest and West Germanic**<br>PNWGmc U Lowering: *róstaz<br>EAF Final Z Deletion: *rósta<br><br>**Early Anglo-Frisian**<br>[no change] | **Old English**<br>PWGmc Final Bare A Loss: *róst |



### Orthography & surface

Outcome: rost



### Lexeme report

#### Reconstruction and comparative evidence

Orel gives both [rustō]{.recon} 'rust' and [rustaz]{.recon} 'rust',
with masculine/feminine alternatives and Old English
[rust]{.iv lang=oe sort=rust role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:23" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:23:1"} 'rust', Old Saxon
[rost]{.iv lang=os sort=rost role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:24" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:24:1"} 'rust' and Old High German [rost]{.iv lang=ohg sort=rost role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:24" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:24:2"} 'rust' [@Orel2003, p. 309].
The conventional feminine *ō*-stem and masculine *az*-stem are distinct
morphological alternatives within his account. Ringe independently reconstructs
[rustaz]{.recon} 'rust'; his discussion of alternative suffixal and cluster
histories for _-st-_ concerns the formation, not the explanation of retained
*u* [@Ringe2017, pp. 247--248, especially p. 248].

The masculine Old English noun favors the masculine citation reconstruction,
selected here as [rústaz]{.recon} 'rust' with the project's acute stress notation.
This is a morphological and comparative choice, not an exclusive disagreement
between Orel and Ringe or a claim that Orel's *ō*-stem is unsupported.
The comparative lowered forms provide context, but output agreement does not
choose a stem class: both formations regularly predict [rost]{.recon form_role=regular_output}
'rust', not the attested Old English noun.

#### Old English evidence

The noun is attested, not reconstructed. Clark Hall gives
[rūst]{.iv lang=oe sort=rust role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:42" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:42:1"} 'rust' (m.)
[@ClarkHall1960, p. 245], and Toller's supplement to Bosworth-Toller records
[rúst]{.iv lang=oe sort=rúst role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:44" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:44:1"} 'rust', beside *rust*,
with a qualification about quantity [@Toller1921, p. 691].
Their lexicographical signs are retained as source spellings; the selected
comparator remains normalized *rust*, without a newly adopted quantity
interpretation. The evidence establishes the noun and masculine gender,
not an independent explanation of its retained vowel.

#### Development to Old English

[SC017 PNWGmcULowering](#rule-PNWGmcULowering) regularly lowers the root
*u* of [rústaz]{.recon} 'rust' before the following non-high vowel, giving
[róstaz]{.recon form_role=regular_output} 'rust'. Final-*z* loss and subsequent
regular developments complete the predicted [rost]{.recon form_role=regular_output}
'rust', not the attested comparator *rust*. Campbell describes the lowering
and its unresolved retained-*u* residues [@Campbell1959, p. 43, §115];
Ringe and Taylor discuss the remaining exceptions without establishing a
general consonantal exemption [@RingeTaylor2014, pp. 27--34].

The control [frústą]{.recon} 'frost' regularly gives
[forst]{.iv lang=oe sort=forst role=comparison_form source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:63" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:63:1"} 'frost', so _-st-_ cannot
be exempted generally. Selecting the masculine stem is not a phonological
repair: *rust* remains an unexplained lexical retention and the mismatch
total does not fall. No exception is inserted into the sound law.

#### Paradigm comparison

Fresh production and isolated-wrapper diagnostics give the following
results. The oblique-type constructions test conditioning; they are not
newly attested Proto-Germanic paradigm cells.

| Form | Role | Result |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| [rústaz]{.recon .iv lang=pgmc sort=rustaz role=selected_input source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:76" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:76:1"} | Selected masculine citation form | [rost]{.recon form_role=regular_output}, mismatch |
| [rústō]{.recon .iv lang=pgmc sort=rusto role=source_protoform source_ref="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:77" occ_id="Germanic/docs/lexeme_reports/model_entries/2162-rust-rust.model.md:77:1"} | Orel's alternative *ō*-stem | [rost]{.recon form_role=regular_output}, mismatch |
| [rústas]{.recon} | Genitive-singular-type probe | [rostes]{.recon form_role=regular_output} |
| [rústai]{.recon} | Dative-singular-type probe | [roste]{.recon form_role=regular_output} |
| [rústi]{.recon} | High-front-vowel diagnostic | [ryst]{.recon form_role=regular_output} |
| [rústu]{.recon} | Marginal high-vowel diagnostic construction | [rust]{.recon form_role=regular_output} |

The hypothetical instrumental-type [rústu]{.recon} 'rust' is a diagnostic construction,
not an attested Proto-Germanic citation form or an adopted historical solution.
Its matching output does not establish a source for the ordinary citation noun.

PROTO: *wúllō
EXPECTED: wull
OUTPUTS: woll



### Proto-Germanic consonant inheritance

English Proto Input: *wúllō

| Earlier Germanic developments | Old English developments |
|:---|:---|
| **Northwest and West Germanic**<br>PNWGmc U Lowering: *wóllō<br>PNWGmc Final Long O Raising: *wóllu<br><br>**Early Anglo-Frisian**<br>[no change] | **Old English**<br>OE High Vowel Apocope: *wóll |

Outcome: woll



### Lexeme report

#### Project note

Original TSV note: DOCUMENTED EXCEPTION (per §17.10.34): regular sound change from *wúllō gives **woll (Stiles 2012 §4.1.1.2 env. (b): low *ō triggers a-umlaut). Campbell §115 explicitly lists 'full' as an exact morphological parallel exception, with OHG 'foll' as the regular outcome. Unlike wulf/fugol/bucc/rust, wull is a fem. ō-stem with no paradigm cell containing high *i/*ī/*j to escape via env. (a) — every cell has a back-vowel ending. The FST output 'woll' is the correct regular result; attested 'wull' is non-derivable in this FST.
